\bar{Z}(w)\Vert _{L_{p_1,p_2}^{\mathscr {G}}}+ \Vert F_{q,m}^{(2)}(s,y,Z,\bar{Z})\Vert _{L_{p_1,p_2}^{\mathscr {G}}}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Σq,m(2)(s,y,Z,Z¯)‖Lp1,p2G≲sm/4supw∈T‖Z(w)-Z¯(w)‖Lp1,p2G+‖Fq,m(2)(s,y,Z,Z¯)‖Lp1,p2G.We can bound A by applying this result (with m:=n-1m:=n-1) as follows:5.13\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\Vert A(Z,\bar{Z})\Vert _{L_{p_1,p_2}^{\mathscr {G}}} \nonumber \\&\quad \lesssim \big \Vert p_{t-\cdot }(x,\cdot )\big \Vert \Sigma _{q,n-1}^{(2)}(\cdot ,\cdot ,Z,\bar{Z})\big \Vert _{L_{p_1,p_2}^{\mathscr {G}}} \textbf{1}_{[0,t]}\big \Vert _{H} \nonumber \\&\quad \lesssim \Big \Vert p_{t-\cdot }(x,\cdot )\sup _{(\theta ,\zeta ) \in [0,t]\times \mathbb {T}}\Big ( \theta ^{\frac{n-1}{4}}\sup _{w \in \mathbb {T}}\Vert Z(w) -\bar{Z}(w)\Vert _{L_{p_1,p_2}^{\mathscr {G}}}\nonumber \\&\quad \quad + \Vert F_{q,n-1}^{(2)}(\theta ,\zeta ,Z,\bar{Z})\Vert _{L_{p_1,p_2}^{\mathscr {G}}} \Big )\textbf{1}_{[0,t]}\Big \Vert _H \nonumber \\&\quad \lesssim t^{n/4}\sup _{w \in \mathbb {T}}\Vert Z(w) -\bar{Z}(w)\Vert _{L_{p_1,p_2}^\mathscr {G}} , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖A(Z,Z¯)‖Lp1,p2G≲‖pt-·(x,·)‖Σq,n-1(2)(·,·,Z,Z¯)‖Lp1,p2G1[0,t]‖H≲‖pt-·(x,·)sup(θ,ζ)∈[0,t]×T(θn-14supw∈T‖Z(w)-Z¯(w)‖Lp1,p2G+‖Fq,n-1(2)(θ,ζ,Z,Z¯)‖Lp1,p2G)1[0,t]‖H≲tn/4supw∈T‖Z(w)-Z¯(w)‖Lp1,p2G,where for the last inequality we used the induction hypothesis. Now we also bound B. \bar{Z}(w)\Vert _{L_{p_1,p_2}^{\mathscr {G}}}^2 + \int _0^t \int _{\mathbb {T}} p_{t-r}^2(x,y)\Vert F_{q,n}^{(2)}(r,y,Z,\bar{Z})\Vert _{L_{p_1,p_2}^{\mathscr {G}}}^2 dydr. \quad \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖B(Z,Z¯)‖Lp1,p2G2≲∫0t∫Tpt-r2(x,y)‖Σq,n(2)(r,y,Z,Z¯)‖Lp1,p2G2dydr≲∫0t∫Tpt-r2(x,y)(tn/4supw∈T‖Z(w)-Z¯(w)‖Lp1,p2G+‖Fq,n(2)(r,y,Z,Z¯)‖Lp1,p2G)2dydr≲t(n+1)/2supw∈T‖Z(w)-Z¯(w)‖Lp1,p2G2+∫0t∫Tpt-r2(x,y)‖Fq,n(2)(r,y,Z,Z¯)‖Lp1,p2G2dydr.By putting the bounds (5.13) and (5.14) into (5.11) we can see that5.15\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert F_{q,n}^{(2)}(t,x,Z,\bar{Z})\Vert _{L_{p_1,p_2}^{\mathscr {G}}}^2&\lesssim t^{n/2}\sup _{w \in \mathbb {T}}\Vert Z(w) - Now going back to (5.15), if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert Z\Vert _{\mathbb {B}}, \Vert \bar{Z}\Vert _{\mathbb {B}} \le N$$\end{document}‖Z‖B,‖Z¯‖B≤N for some given N >0N>0 then the desired result follows by Lemma 11.4. For the general case we can repeat the truncation argument from the n=0n=0 case to finish the proof. □ □

Nondegeneracy of convex combinations of solutions

Throughout the section we assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma \in C^1$$\end{document}σ∈C1 such that there exists a constant μ >0μ>0 such that for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x \in \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}x∈R we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma (x) \ge \mu $$\end{document}σ(x)≥μ. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi ^1,\dots , \phi ^K$$\end{document}ϕ1,⋯,ϕK solve the driftless equation (5.1) with initial conditions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi ^1_0,\dots , \phi ^K_0 \in C(\mathbb {T})$$\end{document}ϕ01,⋯,ϕ0K∈C(T) respectively. Consider the convex combination5.16\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Theta (t,x) := \sum _{i=1}^K c_i \phi ^i(t,x). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Θ(t,x):=∑i=1Kciϕi(t,x).with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ c_1,\dots , c_K \in [0,1]$$\end{document}c1,⋯,cK∈[0,1] satisfying \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sum _{i=1}^K c_i =1$$\end{document}∑i=1Kci=1. For a smooth map g and a nonnegative integer n, we aim to obtain estimates on the expectation of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nabla ^n g(\Theta (t,x))$$\end{document}∇ng(Θ(t,x)) which depend only on a Besov–Hölder norm of g with a negative index, see Lemma 5.10 below.

The following lemma quantifies Theorem 4.5 in the chapter by Nualart in [12].

Lemma 5.7

For any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p \in (2,\infty )$$\end{document}p∈(2,∞) there exists some constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N = N(p,\Vert \sigma \Vert _{C^1}, \mu , K)$$\end{document}N=N(p,‖σ‖C1,μ,K), such that for all t ∈ [0,1]t∈[0,1] we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sup _{x \in \mathbb {T}}\mathbb {E}[\Vert \mathscr {D}\Theta (t,x)\Vert _H^{-p} ]\le N t^{-p/4}.$$\end{document}supx∈TE[‖DΘ(t,x)‖H-p]≤Nt-p/4.

Hence by Lemma 11.4 it follows that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {E}\Vert \mathscr {D} \phi ^i(t,x)\Vert _{L_2([t-\delta ,t]\times \mathbb {T})}^q \lesssim \delta ^{q/4}.$$\end{document}E‖Dϕi(t,x)‖L2([t-δ,t]×T)q≲δq/4.Applying this with q =2pq=2p to bound \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$H_i$$\end{document}Hi, we get5.19\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} H_i \lesssim \int _{t-\delta }^t \int _{\mathbb {T}}p_{t-r}(x,y) \delta ^{p/2}dydr \lesssim \delta ^{p/2+1}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Hi≲∫t-δt∫Tpt-r(x,y)δp/2dydr≲δp/2+1.Now putting (5.19) into (5.18), we get \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {E}|I_B^\delta |^p \lesssim \delta ^p$$\end{document}E|IBδ|p≲δp. Putting this into (5.17) we see that for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} c_0\delta ^{1/2})$$\end{document}ε∈(0,c0δ1/2), we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Gk+1(t,x)=δ(wt,xGk(t,x)).Then by Proposition 4.2, for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} Lemma 5.8

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p \in [1,\infty )$$\end{document}p∈[1,∞), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \in \mathbb {Z}_{\ge 0}$$\end{document}n∈Z≥0, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma \in C^{n+1}$$\end{document}σ∈Cn+1. Then there exists a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N = N(p,n, \Vert \sigma \Vert _{C^{n+1}}, \mu )$$\end{document}N=N(p,n,‖σ‖Cn+1,μ) such that for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(t,x) \in [0,1]\times \mathbb {T}$$\end{document}(t,x)∈[0,1]×T, we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \mathscr {D}^n w_{t,x}\Vert _{L_p(\Omega ;H^{\otimes (n+1)})} \le N t^{(n-1)/4}.$$\end{document}‖Dnwt,x‖Lp(Ω;H⊗(n+1))≤Nt(n-1)/4.

Proof

Fix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(t,x) \in [0,1]\times \mathbb {T}$$\end{document}(t,x)∈[0,1]×T and let X := Θ (t,x)X:=Θ(t,x), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Y := \Vert \mathscr {D}\Theta (t,x)\Vert _H^2$$\end{document}Y:=‖DΘ(t,x)‖H2 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$w := (\mathscr {D}X)/Y$$\end{document}w:=(DX)/Y. We may assume that p>2p>2. By Lemma 5.1, Lemma 5.5 and Lemma 5.7 respectively, we haveTherefore by Lemma 4.5 (with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon := t^{1/4}$$\end{document}ε:=t1/4 and c = 2c=2) to obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \mathscr {D}^n w\Vert _{L_p(\Omega ; H^{\otimes (n+1)})} \lesssim (t^{1/4})^{n+1 -2} \lesssim t^{(n-1)/4}$$\end{document}‖Dnw‖Lp(Ω;H⊗(n+1))≲(t1/4)n+1-2≲t(n-1)/4as required. □ □

Lemma 5.9

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \in \mathbb {Z}_{\ge 0}$$\end{document}n∈Z≥0, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma \in C^n$$\end{document}σ∈Cn. Then for each \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k,m \in \mathbb {Z}_{\ge 0}$$\end{document}k,m∈Z≥0 such that k+m łe nk+m≤n and for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p \in [1,\infty )$$\end{document}p∈[1,∞) there exists a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N = N(k,m,p, \Vert \sigma \Vert _{C^n}, \mu )$$\end{document}N=N(k,m,p,‖σ‖Cn,μ) such that with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q := 2^m p$$\end{document}q:=2mp we have for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(t,x) \in [0,1]\times \mathbb {T}$$\end{document}(t,x)∈[0,1]×T that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\Vert G_m(t,x) \Vert _{\mathscr {W}_p^k} \le Nt^{-m/4}\Vert G_0(t,x)\Vert _{\mathscr {W}_{q}^{k+m}}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Gm(t,x)‖Wpk≤Nt-m/4‖G0(t,x)‖Wqk+m.

Proof

For notational convenience, fix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(t,x) \in [0,1]\times \mathbb {T}$$\end{document}(t,x)∈[0,1]×T, set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$w := w_{t,x}$$\end{document}w:=wt,x, and for i=1,… ,ni=1,⋯,n set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_i:= G_i(t,x)$$\end{document}Gi:=Gi(t,x). The proof will be done by induction with respect to the m variable. For m = 0m=0 the statement is obviously true. Now suppose that the statement is true for some m łe n-1m≤n-1. That is, we suppose that for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$l \in \mathbb {Z}_{\ge 0}$$\end{document}l∈Z≥0 such that l+m łe nl+m≤n we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert G_m\Vert _{\mathscr {W}_p^l} \lesssim t^{-m/4}\Vert G_0\Vert _{\mathscr {W}_{2^m p}^{l+m}}.$$\end{document}‖Gm‖Wpl≲t-m/4‖G0‖W2mpl+m.We show that the statement is also true for m+1m+1, i.e. that for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k \in \mathbb {Z}_{\ge 0}$$\end{document}k∈Z≥0 such that k+(m+1) łe nk+(m+1)≤n, we have5.20\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert G_{m+1}\Vert _{\mathscr {W}_p^k} \lesssim t^{-(m+1)/4}\Vert G_0\Vert _{\mathscr {W}_{2^{m+1}p}^{k+m+1}}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Gm+1‖Wpk≲t-(m+1)/4‖G0‖W2m+1pk+m+1.Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k \in \mathbb {Z}_{\ge 0}$$\end{document}k∈Z≥0, such that k+m+1 łe nk+m+1≤n. Since the divergence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta : \mathscr {W}_p^{k+1} \rightarrow \mathscr {W}_p^k$$\end{document}δ:Wpk+1→Wpk is a bounded linear operator (see [31, Proposition 1.5.7 and point 1 of remarks of Chapter 1]), we have5.21\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert G_{m+1}\Vert _{\mathscr {W}_p^k} = \Vert \delta (w G_{m}) \Vert _{\mathscr {W}_p^k}&\lesssim \Vert w G_{m} \Vert _{\mathscr {W}_p^{k+1}(H)}\nonumber \\ &\lesssim \sum _{i=0}^{k+1} \Vert \mathscr {D}^i(wG_{m}) \Vert _{L_p(\Omega ;H^{\otimes (i+1)})} \nonumber \\&\lesssim \sum _{i=0}^{k+1}\sum _{\lambda _1 + \lambda _2 = i} \Vert \mathscr {D}^{\lambda _1}w\Vert _{L_{2p}(\Omega ; H^{\otimes (\lambda _1 +1)})}\Vert \mathscr {D}^{\lambda _2} G_m\Vert _{L_{2p}(\Omega ; H^{\otimes \lambda _2})} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Gm+1‖Wpk=‖δ(wGm)‖Wpk≲‖wGm‖Wpk+1(H)≲∑i=0k+1‖Di(wGm)‖Lp(Ω;H⊗(i+1))≲∑i=0k+1∑λ1+λ2=i‖Dλ1w‖L2p(Ω;H⊗(λ1+1))‖Dλ2Gm‖L2p(Ω;H⊗λ2).By Lemma 5.8, and since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _1\ge 0$$\end{document}λ1≥0, we have5.22\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert \mathscr {D}^{\lambda _1}w\Vert _{L_{2p}(\Omega ; H^{\otimes (\lambda _1 +1)})} \lesssim t^{(\lambda _1 -1)/4} \lesssim t^{-1/4}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Dλ1w‖L2p(Ω;H⊗(λ1+1))≲t(λ1-1)/4≲t-1/4.Moreover since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _2 + m \le k+1+m \le n$$\end{document}λ2+m≤k+1+m≤n, by the induction hypothesis we have5.23\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert \mathscr {D}^{\lambda _2} G_m\Vert _{L_{2p}(\Omega ; H^{\otimes \lambda _2})} \le \Vert G_m\Vert _{\mathscr {W}_{2p}^{\lambda _2}} \lesssim t^{-m/4}\Vert G_0\Vert _{\mathscr {W}_{2^{m +1} p}^{\lambda _2+m}} \le t^{-m/4}\Vert G_0\Vert _{\mathscr {W}_{2^{m+1}p}^{k+1+m}} . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Dλ2Gm‖L2p(Ω;H⊗λ2)≤‖Gm‖W2p2≲t-m/4‖G0‖W2m+1pλ2+m≤t-m/4‖G0‖W2m+1pk+1+m.Now putting (5.22) and (5.23) into (5.21), we get\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert G_{m+1}\Vert _{\mathscr {W}_p^k} \lesssim t^{-1/4}t^{-m/4}\Vert G_0\Vert _{\mathscr {W}_{2^{m+1}p}^{k+1+m}}.$$\end{document}‖Gm+1‖Wpk≲t-1/4t-m/4‖G0‖W2m+1pk+1+m.Hence (5.20) holds, and the proof is finished. □ □

Lemma 5.10

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \in \mathbb {Z}_{\ge 0}$$\end{document}n∈Z≥0, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma \in C^{n+1}$$\end{document}σ∈Cn+1, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta \in (-2,-1)\cup (-1,0)$$\end{document}β∈(-2,-1)∪(-1,0), and set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q:= 2^{n+2}\textbf{1}_{(-1,0)}(\beta ) + 2^{n+3}\textbf{1}_{(-2,-1)}(\beta ) $$\end{document}q:=2n+21(-1,0)(β)+2n+31(-2,-1)(β) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m := (n+1)\textbf{1}_{(-1,0)}(\beta ) + (n+2)\textbf{1}_{(-2,-1)}(\beta )$$\end{document}m:=(n+1)1(-1,0)(β)+(n+2)1(-2,-1)(β). There exists a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N = N(n,\beta , \Vert \sigma \Vert _{C^{n+1}}, \mu )$$\end{document}N=N(n,β,‖σ‖Cn+1,μ) such that for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g \in C^\infty $$\end{document}g∈C∞, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(t,x) \in [0,1]\times \mathbb {T}$$\end{document}(t,x)∈[0,1]×T, we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\big |\mathbb {E}\big (\nabla ^n g(\Theta (t,x)) G_0(t,x)\big )\big | \le N\Vert g\Vert _{C^\beta } t^{\frac{\beta -n}{4}}\Vert G_0(t,x)\Vert _{\mathscr {W}_{q}^{m}}.$$\end{document}|E(∇ng(Θ(t,x))G0(t,x))|≤N‖g‖Cβtβ-n4‖G0(t,x)‖Wqm.

Proof

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f \in C^\infty $$\end{document}f∈C∞ be the solution of (1-Δ )f = g(1-Δ)f=g and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(t,x) \in [0,1]\times \mathbb {T}$$\end{document}(t,x)∈[0,1]×T. By Proposition 4.2 and by the definition of f, we get\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} |\mathbb {E}(\nabla ^n g(\Theta (t,x)) G_0(t,x))|&= |\mathbb {E}( g(\Theta (t,x)) G_n(t,x))|\\&\le | \mathbb {E}(f(\Theta (t,x))G_n(t,x))|\\&\quad +|\mathbb {E}(\Delta f(\Theta (t,x))G_n(t,x))| =:A+B. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|E(∇ng(Θ(t,x))G0(t,x))|=|E(g(Θ(t,x))Gn(t,x))|≤|E(f(Θ(t,x))Gn(t,x))|+|E(Δf(Θ(t,x))Gn(t,x))|=:A+B.It follows easily from Lemma 5.9 and Proposition 11.8 that5.24\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} A \le \Vert f\Vert _{\mathbb {B}}\Vert G_n(t,x)\Vert _{L_1}&\le \Vert f\Vert _{C^{2+\beta }}t^{-n/4}\Vert G_0(t,x)\Vert _{\mathscr {W}_{2^n}^n}\nonumber \\ &\lesssim \Vert g\Vert _{C^{\beta }} t^{-n/4 + \beta /4}\Vert G_0(t,x)\Vert _{\mathscr {W}_{q}^{m}}.\quad \quad \end{aligned}$$\end{document}A≤‖f‖B‖Gn(t,x)‖L1≤‖f‖C2+βt-n/4‖G0(t,x)‖W2nn≲‖g‖Cβt-n/4+β/4‖G0(t,x)‖Wqm.It remains to be shown that the desired bound also holds on B. To this end, we first note that by Jensen’s inequality and by the BDG inequality for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma \in (0,1)$$\end{document}γ∈(0,1) we have5.25\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Big \Vert \Big (\Theta (t,x) - \sum _{i=1}^K c_i P_t \phi ^i(0,\cdot )(x)\Big )^{\gamma }\Big \Vert _{L_2}&\le \Big \Vert \sum _{i=1}^K c_i\Big (\phi ^i(t,x) - P_t \phi ^i(0,\cdot )(x)\Big )\Big \Vert _{L_{2}}^{\gamma }\nonumber \\&\le \sum _{i=1}^K \Big \Vert \int _0^t \int _{\mathbb {T}} p_{t-r}(x,y)\sigma (\phi ^i(r,y)) \xi (dy,dr)\Big \Vert _{L_{2}}^{\gamma } \nonumber \\&\lesssim t^{\gamma /4}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖(Θ(t,x)-∑i=1KciPtϕi(0,·)(x))γ‖L2≤‖∑i=1Kci(ϕi(t,x)-Ptϕi(0,·)(x))‖L2γ≤∑i=1K‖∫0t∫Tpt-r(x,y)σ(ϕi(r,y))ξ(dy,dr)‖L2γ≲tγ/4.We first consider the case that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta \in (-1,0)$$\end{document}β∈(-1,0). By Proposition 4.2, the fact that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {E}G_{n+1} =0$$\end{document}EGn+1=0, and (5.25) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma = 1+\beta \in (0,1)$$\end{document}γ=1+β∈(0,1), we get5.26\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} B&= |\mathbb {E}(\nabla f(\Theta (t,x))G_{n+1}(t,x))| \nonumber \\&= \Big |\mathbb {E}\Big (\Big (\nabla f(\Theta (t,x)) - \nabla f\Big (\sum _{i=1}^K c_i P_t \phi ^i(0,\cdot )(x)\Big )\Big ) G_{n+1}(t,x))\Big )\Big |\nonumber \\&\lesssim \Vert \nabla f\Vert _{C^{ 1+\beta }}\Big \Vert \Big (\Theta (t,x) - \sum _{i=1}^K c_i P_t \phi ^i(0,\cdot )(x)\Big )^{1+\beta }\Big \Vert _{L_2}\Vert G_{n+1}(t,x)\Vert _{L_2}\nonumber \\&\lesssim \Vert f\Vert _{C^{2+\beta }} t^{\frac{1+\beta }{4}}\Vert G_{n+1}(t,x)\Vert _{L_2}\nonumber \\&\lesssim \Vert g\Vert _{C^\beta }t^{\frac{\beta - n}{4}}\Vert G_0(t,x)\Vert _{\mathscr {W}_{q}^{n+1}}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}B=|E(∇f(Θ(t,x))Gn+1(t,x))|=|E((∇f(Θ(t,x))-∇f(∑i=1KciPtϕi(0,·)(x)))Gn+1(t,x)))|≲‖∇f‖C1+β‖(Θ(t,x)-∑i=1KciPtϕi(0,·)(x))1+β‖L2‖Gn+1(t,x)‖L2≲‖f‖C2+βt1+β4‖Gn+1(t,x)‖L2≲‖g‖Cβtβ-n4‖G0(t,x)‖Wqn+1,where for the last inequality we used Proposition 11.8 and Lemma 5.9. We now also deal with the case when \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta \in (-2,-1)$$\end{document}β∈(-2,-1). Let s \ge 0s≥0 and suppose that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Z:\Omega \times \mathbb {T} \rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}Z:Ω×T→R is an \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {F}_s \otimes \mathscr {B}(\mathbb {T})$$\end{document}Fs⊗B(T)-measurable map, such that Z(x) is continuous in x and that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sup _{x \in \mathbb {T}}\Vert Z(x)\Vert _{L_2} <\infty $$\end{document}supx∈T‖Z(x)‖L2<∞. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi ^{Z,s}$$\end{document}ϕZ,s denote the solution of5.29\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} (\partial _t - \Delta )\phi ^{Z,s} = \sigma (\phi ^{Z,s})\xi \qquad \text {in }(s, 1) \times \mathbb {T}, \qquad \phi ^{Z,s}_s = Z. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(∂t-Δ)ϕZ,s=σ(ϕZ,s)ξin(s,1)×T,ϕsZ,s=Z.For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(t,x) \in [s,1]\times \mathbb {T}$$\end{document}(t,x)∈[s,1]×T, the solution satisfies the integral equation5.30\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \phi ^{Z,s}(t,x) = P_{t-s}Z(x) + \int _s^t \int _{\mathbb {T}}p_{t-r}(x,y) \sigma (\phi ^{Z,s}(r,y)) \xi (dy,dr). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ϕZ,s(t,x)=Pt-sZ(x)+∫st∫Tpt-r(x,y)σ(ϕZ,s(r,y))ξ(dy,dr).We will use the shorthand5.31\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \phi ^Z(t,x) := \phi ^{Z,0}(t,x). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ϕZ(t,x):=ϕZ,0(t,x).

Lemma 5.11

Proof

By Lemma 11.11 we have5.32\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Big |\mathbb {E}^s\Big ( \nabla ^n g\big (\Phi ^{Z_1,\dots , Z_K,s}(t,x)\big ) h\big (\phi ^{Z_1,s}(t,x),\dots , \phi ^{Z_K,s}(t,x)\big ) \Big )\Big | = G(Z_1,\dots ,Z_K), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|Es(∇ng(ΦZ1,⋯,ZK,s(t,x))h(ϕZ1,s(t,x),⋯,ϕZK,s(t,x)))|=G(Z1,⋯,ZK),where for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$z_1, \dots , z_K \in C(\mathbb {T})$$\end{document}z1,⋯,zK∈C(T) we define\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G(z_1,\dots ,z_K) := \Big |\mathbb {E}\Big (\nabla ^n g\Big (\sum _{i=1}^K c_i \phi ^{z_i}(t-s,x)\Big ) h(\phi ^{z_i}(t-s,x),\dots , \phi ^{z_K}(t-s,x))\Big )\Big |.$$\end{document}G(z1,⋯,zK):=|E(∇ng(∑i=1Kciϕzi(t-s,x))h(ϕzi(t-s,x),⋯,ϕzK(t-s,x)))|.By Lemma 5.10 we have5.33Now putting (5.33) into (5.32), the desired result follows. □ □

Driftless approximation

In this section, we establish error estimates for the approximation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi ^{u(s, \cdot ),s}(t,x)$$\end{document}ϕu(s,·),s(t,x) to the solution u(t, x). The main results of this section are Lemma 6.4 and Lemma 6.6.

Lemma 6.1

Let u be a regularised solution of (1.1) with initial condition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u(0,\cdot ) = u_0 \in C(\mathbb {T})$$\end{document}u(0,·)=u0∈C(T) and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p \in [2,\infty )$$\end{document}p∈[2,∞). There exists a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N = N(p,\Vert \sigma \Vert _{\mathbb {B}})$$\end{document}N=N(p,‖σ‖B) such that for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(t,x) \in [0,1]\times \mathbb {T}$$\end{document}(t,x)∈[0,1]×T we have6.1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert u(t,x)\Vert _{L_p} \le N\Big (\Vert u_0\Vert _{\mathbb {B}(\mathbb {T})}+ \Vert D^u_t(x)\Vert _{L_p} + t^{1/4}\Big ). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖u(t,x)‖Lp≤N(‖u0‖B(T)+‖Dtu(x)‖Lp+t1/4).Consequently, if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u \in \mathscr {U}_p^0$$\end{document}u∈Up0, then6.2\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \sup _{(t,x) \in [0,1]\times \mathbb {T}}\Vert u(t,x)\Vert _{L_p} <\infty . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}sup(t,x)∈[0,1]×T‖u(t,x)‖Lp<∞.

Proof

From (3.2) and the BDG inequality we can see that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\Vert u(t,x)\Vert _{L_p}^2&\lesssim \Vert u_0\Vert _{\mathbb {B}(\mathbb {T})}^2 + \Vert D_t^u(x)\Vert _{L_p}^2 + \int _0^t \int _{\mathbb {T}}p_{t-r}^2(x,y)\Vert \sigma \Vert _{\mathbb {B}}^2dydr, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖u(t,x)‖Lp2≲‖u0‖B(T)2+‖Dtu(x)‖Lp2+∫0t∫Tpt-r2(x,y)‖σ‖B2dydr,and thus the inequality (6.1) follows. Now suppose that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u \in \mathscr {U}_p^0$$\end{document}u∈Up0. Then noting that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D_t^u = D_t^u - P_{t-0}D_0^u$$\end{document}Dtu=Dtu-Pt-0D0u, we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sup _{(t,x) \in [0,1]\times \mathbb {T}}\Vert D^u_t(x)\Vert _{L_p} \le [D^u]_{\mathscr {V}_p^0} <\infty .$$\end{document}sup(t,x)∈[0,1]×T‖Dtu(x)‖Lp≤[Du]Vp0<∞.Also, note that by Assumption 3.1 the initial condition u(0,· )u(0,·) is bounded. Therefore all terms on the right hand side of (6.1) are bounded in (t, x), and thus (6.2) follows. □ □

Recall that the random fields \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi ^{z,s}(t,x)$$\end{document}ϕz,s(t,x) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\phi ^z(t,x)$$\end{document}ϕz(t,x) are defined by (5.30) and (5.31) respectively. Remark 6.2

Note that for all s ∈ [0,1]s∈[0,1], by definition, the random field \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^i(s,x)$$\end{document}ui(s,x) is continuous in x, and by Lemma 6.1 we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sup _{x \in \mathbb {T}}\Vert u^i(s,x)\Vert _{L_p} <\infty $$\end{document}supx∈T‖ui(s,x)‖Lp<∞. Consequentially, the expression (6.4) is well-defined.

For brevity, we will use the convention\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${[}u^1,u^2]_{\mathscr {S}_p^{\beta }} := [u^1,u^2]_{\mathscr {S}_p^{\beta }[0,1]} \quad \text {and}\quad [f]_{\mathscr {V}_p^\beta } :=[f]_{\mathscr {V}_p^\beta [0,1]},$$\end{document}[u1,u2]Spβ:=[u1,u2]Spβ[0,1]and[f]Vpβ:=[f]Vpβ[0,1],where the last notation is defined in Definition 3.4. By the triangle inequality and by Lemma 5.6 the following result holds:

Lemma 6.3

Lemma 6.4

Assumption 6.5

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha \in (-1,0)$$\end{document}α∈(-1,0), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$b \in C^\alpha $$\end{document}b∈Cα, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \in \mathbb {Z}_{\ge 0}$$\end{document}n∈Z≥0, and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma \in C^{n+2}$$\end{document}σ∈Cn+2 such that there exists a constant μ > 0μ>0 such that for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x \in \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}x∈R, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma ^2(x) > \mu ^2$$\end{document}σ2(x)>μ2. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta \in [\frac{1}{2} ,1+\frac{\alpha }{4}]$$\end{document}β∈[12,1+α4], and suppose that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^1,u^2$$\end{document}u1,u2 are regularised solutions of (1.1) in the class \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {U}^\beta $$\end{document}Uβ.

For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(s,a) \in [0,1]_{\le }^2$$\end{document}(s,a)∈[0,1]≤2, consider the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(C(\mathbb {T}))^4$$\end{document}(C(T))4-valued \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {F}_a$$\end{document}Fa-measurable random variable6.9\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} Z :=\Big (\phi ^{u^1(s,\cdot ),s}(a,\cdot ),\ \phi ^{u^2(s,\cdot ),s}(a,\cdot ),\ u^1(a,\cdot ),\ u^2(a,\cdot )\Big ). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Z:=(ϕu1(s,·),s(a,·),ϕu2(s,·),s(a,·),u1(a,·),u2(a,·)).Recall the definitions of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F^{(2)}$$\end{document}F(2) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Sigma ^{(2)}$$\end{document}Σ(2) from (5.7) and (5.8). Moreover for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(t,x) \in [0,1]\times \mathbb {T}$$\end{document}(t,x)∈[0,1]×T and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$z = (z_1,\dots , z_4) \in (C(\mathbb {T}))^4$$\end{document}z=(z1,⋯,z4)∈(C(T))4, define6.106.11By Lemma 5.6, it follows that the expression in (6.10) is continuous in z. Similarly, by Lemma 5.6, the product and chain rule formulas for Malliavin derivatives, it is easy to see that the same holds for the expression in (6.11). Our next task is to obtain an estimate on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F^{(4)}$$\end{document}F(4) evaluated at Z. This estimate is given in the next lemma.

Lemma 6.6

(Four point estimate) Let Assumption 6.5 hold. Then for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p \in [2,\infty )$$\end{document}p∈[2,∞), there exists a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N = N(n,p,\Vert \sigma \Vert _{C^{n+2}}, \alpha , \beta )$$\end{document}N=N(n,p,‖σ‖Cn+2,α,β) such that for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(S,T) \in [0,1]_{\le }^2$$\end{document}(S,T)∈[0,1]≤2, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(s,a) \in [S,T]_{\le }^2$$\end{document}(s,a)∈[S,T]≤2, t ∈ [0, 1 - a+s]t∈[0,1-a+s] and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x \in \mathbb {T}$$\end{document}x∈T, we have that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert F^{(4)}_{p,n}(t,\,x,Z)\Vert _{L_{p}} \le \,&N (1+\max _{i \in \{1,2\}}[D^{u^i}]_{\mathscr {V}_{2p}^\beta })\Big ([u^1,u^2]_{\mathscr {S}_p^{1/2}[S,a]} \\&+ \Vert u^1(S,\cdot ) - To prove the above estimate, we will need the following auxiliary lemma.

Lemma 6.7

Let Assumption 6.5 hold. Then for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p \in [2,\infty )$$\end{document}p∈[2,∞) there exists a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N = N(n,p, \Vert \sigma \Vert _{C^{n+2}}, \alpha , \beta )$$\end{document}N=N(n,p,‖σ‖Cn+2,α,β) such that for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(s,a) \in [0,1]_{\le }^2$$\end{document}(s,a)∈[0,1]≤2, t ∈ [0,1-a+s]t∈[0,1-a+s] and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x \in \mathbb {T}$$\end{document}x∈T we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\Vert \Sigma ^{(4)}_{p,n}(t,x,Z)\Vert _{L_{p}}&\le N \max _{i \in \{1,2\}}[D^{u^i}]_{\mathscr {V}_{2p}^\beta }\Vert u^1(s,\cdot ) - u^2(s,\cdot )\Vert _{\mathbb {B}(\mathbb {T}, L_p)} (a-s)^{\beta }\\&\qquad + N\textbf{1}_{n \ge 1}\sum _{i=0}^{n-1} \Vert F^{(4)}_{2p,i}(t,x,Z)\Vert _{L_{p}} + N\Vert F_{p,n}^{(4)}(t,x, Z)\Vert _{L_{p}}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Σp,n(4)(t,x,Z)‖Lp≤Nmaxi∈{1,2}[Di]V2pβ‖u1(s,·)-u2(s,·)‖B(T,Lp)(a-s)β+N1n≥1∑i=0n-1‖F2p,i(4)(t,x,Z)‖Lp+N‖Fp,n(4)(t,x,Z)‖Lp.

We proceed with showing that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert A\Vert _{L_p}$$\end{document}‖A‖Lp can be estimated by the first term of the desired bound. To this end, note that by Lemmata 5.6 and 6.4 we have for l=1,2l=1,2, uniformly in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$j \in \{0,\dots , n\}$$\end{document}j∈{0,⋯,n}, that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \big \Vert F^{(2)}_{4p,j}(t,x,Z_l,Z_{l+2})\big \Vert _{L_{2p,\infty }^{\mathscr {F}_s}}&\lesssim \sup _{w \in \mathbb {T}}\Vert Z_l(w) - Finally, when n=0n=0, using (11.12), we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \tilde{A}\Vert _{L_p}$$\end{document}‖A~‖Lp and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert A\Vert _{L_p}$$\end{document}‖A‖Lp and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert C\Vert _{L_p}$$\end{document}‖C‖Lp respectively, one can show that the desired result also holds for n=0n=0, which finishes the proof. □ □

We are now in position to prove Lemma 6.6.

Proof of Lemma 6.6

We begin by confirming that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sup _{(t,x) \in [0,1]\times \mathbb {T}}\Vert F^{(4)}_{p,n}(t,x,Z)\Vert _{L_{p}} < \infty $$\end{document}sup(t,x)∈[0,1]×T‖Fp,n(4)(t,x,Z)‖Lp<∞. This is indeed true, since by the triangle inequality, Lemma 5.6 and Lemma 6.4 we have6.13\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \sup _{x \in \mathbb {T}}\Vert F^{(4)}_{p,n}(t,x,Z)\Vert _{L_{p}}&\le \sum _{i \in \{1,2\}}\Vert F_{p,n}^{(2)}\big (t,x, \phi ^{u^i(s,\cdot ),s}(a,\cdot ), u^i(a,\cdot )\big )\Vert _{L_{p}} \nonumber \\&\lesssim \sum _{i \in \{1,2\}}t^{\frac{n}{4}}\sup _{x \in \mathbb {T}}\Vert \phi ^{u^i(s,\cdot ),s}(a,x)- u^i(a,x)\Vert _{L_{p}} \nonumber \\ &\lesssim t^{\frac{n}{4}}(a-s)^{\beta }\max _{i \in \{1,2\}}[D^{u^i}]_{\mathscr {V}_p^\beta }. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}supx∈T‖Fp,n(4)(t,x,Z)‖Lp≤∑i∈{1,2}‖Fp,n(2)(t,x,ϕui(s,·),s(a,·),ui(a,·))‖Lp≲∑i∈{1,2}tn4supx∈T‖ϕui(s,·),s(a,x)-ui(a,x)‖Lp≲tn4(a-s)βmaxi∈{1,2}[Di]Vpβ.The rest of the proof will be done by induction.

Step 1: We prove that the statement holds for n=0n=0. By (5.30) we have for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$z=(z_1,...,z_4) \in (C(\mathbb {T}))^4$$\end{document}z=(z1,...,z4)∈(C(T))4 that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&(\phi ^{z_1} - Regularisation estimates

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u,u^1,u^2$$\end{document}u,u1,u2 be regularised solutions of (1.1) with potentially different drift terms, f be a measurable kernel on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(0,1]\times \mathbb {T}$$\end{document}(0,1]×T and g be a smooth function on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathbb {R}}$$\end{document}R. In this section, we obtain quantitative bounds for expressions of the forms \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\int _s^t \int _{\mathbb {T}} f_r(y)g(u(r,y))dydr$$\end{document}∫st∫Tfr(y)g(u(r,y))dydr and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\int _s^t \int _{\mathbb {T}}f_r(y)(g(u^1(r,y))- g(u^2(r,y)))dydr$$\end{document}∫st∫Tfr(y)(g(u1(r,y))-g(u2(r,y)))dydr which depend on a Besov–Hölder norm of g with a negative index.

Lemma 7.1

Let Assumption 3.1 hold and let u be a regularised solution of (1.1). Then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} |\mathbb {E}^s \delta A_{s,a,t}|&= |\mathbb {E}^s(A_{s,t} - Lemma 7.4

Proof

\alpha /4$$\end{document}β>1/2-α/4 and that α >-1α>-1. Consequently, by (7.2) and (7.8), we have that the condition (4.1) is satisfied. In addition, by using Lemma 6.4 and the regularity of g, it is straightforward to see that the process\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathscr {A}_t:=\int _0^t \int _{\mathbb {T}}p_{T-r}(x,y)\big (g(u^1(r,y)) - g(u^2(r,y))\big ) dydr \end{aligned}$$\end{document}At:=∫0t∫TpT-r(x,y)(g(u1(r,y))-g(u2(r,y)))dydrsatisfies (4.2) and (4.3). Consequently, the conclusion follows from Lemma 4.1 and the fact that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(S,T) \in [0,1]_{\le }^2$$\end{document}(S,T)∈[0,1]≤2 was arbitrary. □ □

Corollary 7.5

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p \in [2,\infty )$$\end{document}p∈[2,∞) and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma \in C^4$$\end{document}σ∈C4 such that there exists a constant μ >0μ>0 such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma ^2(x) \ge \mu ^2$$\end{document}σ2(x)≥μ2. For i=1,2i=1,2, let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$b^i \in C^{\alpha }$$\end{document}bi∈Cα and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^i$$\end{document}ui be regularised solutions of\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\partial _t - \Delta )u^i = b^i(u^i) + \sigma (u^i)\xi (dy,dr)$$\end{document}(∂t-Δ)ui=bi(ui)+σ(ui)ξ(dy,dr)in the class \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {U}^\beta $$\end{document}Uβ for some \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta \in \big (\frac{1}{2} - \frac{\alpha }{4}, 1+\frac{\alpha }{4}\big ]$$\end{document}β∈(12-α4,1+α4]. There exists a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N = N(p, \Vert \sigma \Vert _{C^4}, \mu , \alpha , \beta )$$\end{document}N=N(p,‖σ‖C4,μ,α,β) such that for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g^1, g^2 \in C^\infty $$\end{document}g1,g2∈C∞, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(s,t) \in [0,1]^2_{\le }$$\end{document}(s,t)∈[0,1]≤2, we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\Big \Vert \int _s^t \int _{\mathbb {T}} p_{t-r}(x,y)\big (g^1(u^1(r,y)) - g^2(u^2(r,y))\big )dydr\Big \Vert _{L_p}\\&\quad \le N\big (1+\max _{i \in \{1,2\}}[D^{u^i}]_{\mathscr {V}_{2p}^\beta }\big )|t-s|^{(3+\alpha )/4}\\&\qquad \times \Big (\Vert g^1 - g^2\Vert _{C^{\alpha -1}} + \Vert g^2\Vert _{C^\alpha }\Big ([u^1,u^2]_{\mathscr {S}_p^{1/2}[s,t]} + \Vert u^1(s,\cdot ) - u^2(s,\cdot )\Vert _{\mathbb {B}(\mathbb {T}, L_p)}\Big )\Big ). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∫st∫Tpt-r(x,y)(g1(u1(r,y))-g2(u2(r,y)))dydr‖Lp≤N(1+maxi∈{1,2}[Di]V2pβ)|t-s|(3+α)/4×(‖g1-g2‖Cα-1+‖g2‖Cα([u1,u2]Sp1/2[s,t]+‖u1(s,·)-u2(s,·)‖B(T,Lp))).

Proof

Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta >\frac{1}{2} - \frac{\alpha }{4} = \frac{1}{4} - \frac{\alpha -1}{4} $$\end{document}β>12-α4=14-α-14, we can see that the condition of Corollary 7.2 is satisfied with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda = \alpha -1$$\end{document}λ=α-1. The desired result follows from Corollary 7.2 and Lemma 7.4 by the triangle inequality. □ □

The \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {S}_p$$\end{document}Sp-bracket of two solutions

Lemma 8.2

Now the desired result follows by the fact that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(S,T) \in [0,1]_{\le }^2$$\end{document}(S,T)∈[0,1]≤2 was arbitrary. □ □

Lemma 8.3

(Splitting the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {S}_p^{1/2}$$\end{document}Sp1/2-bracket) Let Assumption 8.1 hold and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p \in [2,\infty )$$\end{document}p∈[2,∞). Lemma 8.4

Let Assumption 8.1 hold and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K \in \mathbb {Z}_{\ge 2}$$\end{document}K∈Z≥2, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p \in [2,\infty )$$\end{document}p∈[2,∞). There exists a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N = N(p,\Vert \sigma \Vert _{C^{4}}, K, \mu , \alpha , \beta )$$\end{document}N=N(p,‖σ‖C4,K,μ,α,β) such that with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M : = N(1+ \max _{i \in \{1,2\}} [D^{u^i}]_{\mathscr {V}_{2p}})$$\end{document}M:=N(1+maxi∈{1,2}[Di]V2p) we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${[}u^1,u^2]_{\mathscr {S}_p^{1/2}} \le (K-1)M^{K-1}\Vert u^1_0 - u^2_0\Vert _{\mathbb {B}(\mathbb {T})} + 2\sum _{i=0}^{K-1}M^{i}[u^1,u^2]_{\mathscr {S}^{1/2}_p[\frac{K-i-1}{K}, \frac{K-i}{K}]}.$$\end{document}[u1,u2]Sp1/2≤(K-1)MK-1‖u01-u02‖B(T)+2∑i=0K-1Mi[u1,u2]Sp1/2[K-i-1K,K-iK].

Proof

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$a_{s,t} := [u^1,u^2]_{\mathscr {S}_p^{1/2}[s,t]}$$\end{document}as,t:=[u1,u2]Sp1/2[s,t] and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_0^{1,2} := \Vert u^1_0 - u^2_0\Vert _{\mathbb {B}(\mathbb {T})}$$\end{document}u01,2:=‖u01-u02‖B(T). We will begin by using induction to show that for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \in \{1,\dots , K-1\}$$\end{document}n∈{1,⋯,K-1} we have8.6\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} a_{0,1} \le M^n a_{0,\frac{K-n}{K}} + \Big (\sum _{i=1}^n M^i\Big )u^{1,2}_0+2\sum _{i=0}^{n-1}M^i a_{\frac{K-i -1}{K}, \frac{K-i}{K}}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}a0,1≤Mna0,K-nK+(∑i=1nMi)u01,2+2∑i=0n-1MiaK-i-1K,K-iK.By Lemma 8.3 we have that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$a_{0,1} \le M(a_{0,\frac{K-1}{K}} + u_0^{1,2}) + 2a_{\frac{K-1}{K}, 1},$$\end{document}a0,1≤M(a0,K-1K+u01,2)+2aK-1K,1,therefore (8.6) holds for the initial case n=1n=1. Now suppose that (8.6) holds for some \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \in \{1, \dots , K-2\}$$\end{document}n∈{1,⋯,K-2}. We will show that it also holds for n+1n+1. To this end, we first apply the induction hypothesis and then Lemma 8.3, to get that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} a_{0,1}&\le M^n a_{0,\frac{K-n}{K}} + \Big (\sum _{i=1}^n M^i\Big )u_0^{1,2} + 2\sum _{i=0}^{n-1}M^i a_{\frac{K-i -1}{K}, \frac{K-i}{K}}\\&\le M^n\Big ( M \big (a_{0,\frac{K-n-1}{K}}+ u_0^{1,2}\big )+ 2a_{\frac{K-n-1}{K},\frac{K-n}{K}} \Big ) \\&\qquad +\Big (\sum _{i=1}^n M^i\Big )u_0^{1,2} + 2\sum _{i=0}^{n-1}M^i a_{\frac{K-i -1}{K}, \frac{K-i}{K}}\\&= M^{n+1}a_{0,\frac{K-n-1}{K}} + M^{n+1}u_{0}^{1,2} + \Big (\sum _{i=1}^n M^i\Big )u_0^{1,2} + 2M^n a_{\frac{K-n-1}{K}, \frac{K-n}{K}} \\&\qquad + 2\sum _{i=0}^{n-1}M^i a_{\frac{K-i -1}{K}, \frac{K-i}{K}}\\&= M^{n+1}a_{0,\frac{K-n-1}{K}} + \Big (\sum _{i=1}^{n+1} M^i\Big )u_0^{1,2} + 2\sum _{i=0}^{n}M^i a_{\frac{K-i -1}{K}, \frac{K-i}{K}} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}a0,1≤Mna0,K-nK+(∑i=1nMi)u01,2+2∑i=0n-1MiaK-i-1K,K-iK≤Mn(M(a0,K-n-1K+u01,2)+2aK-n-1K,K-nK)+(∑i=1nMi)u01,2+2∑i=0n-1MiaK-i-1K,K-iK=Mn+1a0,K-n-1K+Mn+1u01,2+(∑i=1nMi)u01,2+2MnaK-n-1K,K-nK+2∑i=0n-1MiaK-i-1K,K-iK=Mn+1a0,K-n-1K+(∑i=1n+1Mi)u01,2+2∑i=0nMiaK-i-1K,K-iKas required. Therefore (8.6) is proven. Now choosing n:=K-1n:=K-1 in (8.6) and assuming without loss of generality that N\ge 1N≥1 and therefore M\ge 1M≥1, we get\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} a_{0,1}&\le \Big (\sum _{i=1}^{K-1} M^i\Big )u_0^{1,2} + M^{K-1}a_{0,\frac{1}{K}} + 2 \sum _{i=0}^{K-2}M^i a_{\frac{K-i -1}{K}, \frac{K-i}{K}}\\&\le (K-1)M^{K-1}u_{0}^{1,2} + 2\sum _{i=0}^{K-1}M^i a_{\frac{K-i -1}{K}, \frac{K-i}{K}} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}a0,1≤(∑i=1K-1Mi)u01,2+MK-1a0,1K+2∑i=0K-2MiaK-i-1K,K-iK≤(K-1)MK-1u01,2+2∑i=0K-1MiaK-i-1K,K-iKas required. □ □

Lemma 8.5

\usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u \in \mathscr {U}_2^\beta $$\end{document}u∈U2β, we have \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[D^u]_{\mathscr {V}_2^\beta } <\infty $$\end{document}[Du]V2β<∞, and thus by the inequality (9.1) we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[D^u]_{\mathscr {V}_p^\beta }<\infty $$\end{document}[Du]Vpβ<∞, and thus \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u \in \mathscr {U}_p^\beta $$\end{document}u∈Upβ. Since p \ge 2p≥2 was arbitrary, it follows that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u \in \mathscr {U}^\beta .$$\end{document}u∈Uβ.

Note that on the right hand side of (9.1), the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[D^u]_{\mathscr {V}_2^\beta [s,t]}$$\end{document}[Du]V2β[s,t] may be replaced with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[D^u]_{\mathscr {V}_p^\beta [s,t]}$$\end{document}[Du]Vpβ[s,t]. Hence choosing sufficiently large \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K \in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}K∈N, it follows that9.2\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \max _{i \in \{0,\dots ,K-1\}}[D^u]_{\mathscr {V}_p^\beta [\frac{i}{K},\frac{i+1}{K}]} \lesssim \Vert b\Vert _{C^\alpha }. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}maxi∈{0,⋯,K-1}[Du]Vpβ[iK,i+1K]≲‖b‖Cα.To this end we may pick \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K := \Big \lceil (2\tilde{N}\Vert b\Vert _{C^\alpha })^{\frac{4}{\alpha +3}} \Big \rceil $$\end{document}K:=⌈(2N~‖b‖Cα)4α+3⌉. Moreover using Lemma 11.9 and the inequality (9.2), we obtain that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \,[D^u]_{\mathscr {V}_p^\beta [0,1]}&\le 2^K\sum _{i=0}^{K-1} [D^u]_{\mathscr {V}_p^\beta [\frac{i}{K},\frac{i+1}{K}]}\\&\lesssim 2^K\sum _{i=0}^{K-1} \Vert b\Vert _{C^\alpha } \lesssim K2^K\Vert b\Vert _{C^\alpha }, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}[Du]Vpβ[0,1]≤2K∑i=0K-1[Du]Vpβ[iK,i+1K]≲2K∑i=0K-1‖b‖Cα≲K2K‖b‖Cα,which finishes the proof . □ □

Lemma 9.2

(The regularity of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D^u$$\end{document}Du) Let Assumption 3.1 hold, and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p \in [2,\infty )$$\end{document}p∈[2,∞), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta \in (\frac{1}{2} - \frac{\alpha }{4}, 1+\frac{\alpha }{4}]$$\end{document}β∈(12-α4,1+α4]. There exists a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N = N(p,\Vert \sigma \Vert _{C^4}, \mu , \alpha , \beta )>0$$\end{document}N=N(p,‖σ‖C4,μ,α,β)>0 such that if u is a regularised solution of class \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {U}^\beta $$\end{document}Uβ, then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert D^u\Vert _{C^{\frac{1}{4},\frac{1}{2}}([0,1]\times \mathbb {T}, L_p)} \le N(1+\Vert b\Vert _{C^\alpha })(1+[D^u]_{\mathscr {V}_p^\beta }).$$\end{document}‖Du‖C14,12([0,1]×T,Lp)≤N(1+‖b‖Cα)(1+[Du]Vpβ).

Proof

Noting that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \cdot \Vert _{L_p} = \Vert \Vert \cdot \Vert _{L_p|\mathscr {F}_s}\Vert _{L_p} \le \Vert \Vert \cdot \Vert _{L_p|\mathscr {F}_s}\Vert _{L_\infty }$$\end{document}‖·‖Lp=‖‖·‖Lp|Fs‖Lp≤‖‖·‖Lp|Fs‖L∞ and that from the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D^u$$\end{document}Du (see (3.1)) we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D_0^u =0$$\end{document}D0u=0, we conclude for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(t,x) \in [0,1]\times \mathbb {T}$$\end{document}(t,x)∈[0,1]×T that9.3\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert D_t^u(x)\Vert _{L_p}&\le \Vert D_t^u(x) - P_{t-0}D_0^u(x)\Vert _{L_{p,\infty }^{\mathscr {F}_s}}\le [D^u]_{\mathscr {V}_p^\beta }. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Dtu(x)‖Lp≤‖Dtu(x)-Pt-0D0u(x)‖Lp,∞s≤[Du]Vpβ.Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(b^n)_{n \in \mathbb {N}} \subset C^\infty $$\end{document}(bn)n∈N⊂C∞ be a sequence of smooth functions such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$b^n \rightarrow b$$\end{document}bn→b in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C^\alpha $$\end{document}Cα. By (3.1), Fatou’s lemma and Corollary 7.3, we can see that for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb}
