\usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \sup _{t \in [0,1]}\Vert D^u_t\Vert _{C^{1/2}(\mathbb {T}) }\lesssim (1+\Vert b\Vert _{C^\alpha })(1+[D^u]_{\mathscr {V}_p^\beta }). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}supt∈[0,1]‖Dtu‖C1/2(T)≲(1+‖b‖Cα)(1+[Du]Vpβ).Finally, note that since by assumption we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \frac{1}{4}$$\end{document}β>12-α4>14, and thus9.6\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}[Du]Vp1/4≤[Du]Vpβ.By (9.5) and (9.6), the desired bound holds for the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} L_p)$$\end{document}C0,12([0,1]×T,Lp)-norm and for the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {V}_p^{1/4}$$\end{document}Vp1/4-bracket. Hence by Lemma 11.10 the proof is finished. □ □

Lemma 9.3

(An a priori estimate) Let Assumption 3.1 hold, and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p\in [2,\infty )$$\end{document}p∈[2,∞), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon \in (0, \frac{1}{2})$$\end{document}ε∈(0,12), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta \in (\frac{1}{2} - \frac{\alpha }{4}, 1+\frac{\alpha }{4}]$$\end{document}β∈(12-α4,1+α4]. There exists a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$N = N(p,\Vert \sigma \Vert _{C^4}, \mu , \alpha , \beta , \varepsilon )>0$$\end{document}N=N(p,‖σ‖C4,μ,α,β,ε)>0 such that if u is a regularised solution of class \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {U}^\beta $$\end{document}Uβ, then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}\,\Vert u -P_\cdot u_0(\cdot )\Vert _{C^{1/4 - \varepsilon /2, 1/2-\varepsilon }([0,1]\times \mathbb {T}, L_p)} \le N(1+\Vert b\Vert _{C^\alpha })(1+[D^u]_{\mathscr {V}_p^\beta }). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖u-P·u0(·)‖C1/4-ε/2,1/2-ε([0,1]×T,Lp)≤N(1+‖b‖Cα)(1+[Du]Vpβ).

Proof

For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(t,x) \in [0,1]\times \mathbb {T}$$\end{document}(t,x)∈[0,1]×T denote\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V_t(x) :=\int _0^t \int _{\mathbb {T}} p_{t-r}(x,y) \sigma (u(r,y)) \xi (dy,dr).$$\end{document}Vt(x):=∫0t∫Tpt-r(x,y)σ(u(r,y))ξ(dy,dr).By the triangle inequality\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert u - Pu_0\Vert _{C^{1/4 - \varepsilon /2, 1/2-\varepsilon }([0,1]\times \mathbb {T},L_p)} \le \,&\Vert D^u\Vert _{C^{1/4 - \varepsilon /2, 1/2-\varepsilon }([0,1]\times \mathbb {T},L_p)}\\&+ \Vert V\Vert _{C^{1/4 - \varepsilon /2, 1/2-\varepsilon }([0,1]\times \mathbb {T},L_p)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖u-Pu0‖C1/4-ε/2,1/2-ε([0,1]×T,Lp)≤‖Du‖C1/4-ε/2,1/2-ε([0,1]×T,Lp)+‖V‖C1/4-ε/2,1/2-ε([0,1]×T,Lp).But by Lemma 9.2, we know that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert D^u\Vert _{C^{1/4, 1/2}([0,1]\times \mathbb {T},L_p)} \lesssim (1+\Vert b\Vert _{C^\alpha })(1+[D^u]_{\mathscr {V}_p^\beta })$$\end{document}‖Du‖C1/4,1/2([0,1]×T,Lp)≲(1+‖b‖Cα)(1+[Du]Vpβ) and it can be seen from the BDG inequality and by the heat kernel estimates (11.4) and (11.5) that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert V\Vert _{C^{1/4 - \varepsilon /2, 1/2-\varepsilon }([0,1]\times \mathbb {T}, L_p)} \lesssim 1$$\end{document}‖V‖C1/4-ε/2,1/2-ε([0,1]×T,Lp)≲1, and thus the proof is finished. □ □

Proof of the main result

Theorem 10.1

(Uniqueness) Let Assumption 3.1 hold, let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta \in (\frac{1}{2} - \frac{\alpha }{4}, 1+\frac{\alpha }{4}]$$\end{document}β∈(12-α4,1+α4] and suppose that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^1,u^2$$\end{document}u1,u2 are regularised solutions of (1.1) in the class \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {U}_2^{\beta }$$\end{document}U2β. Then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^1(t,x) = u^2(t,x)$$\end{document}u1(t,x)=u2(t,x) almost surely for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(t,x) \in [0,1]\times \mathbb {T}$$\end{document}(t,x)∈[0,1]×T.

Proof

Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^1,u^2 \in \mathscr {U}_2^\beta $$\end{document}u1,u2∈U2β, it also follows by Lemma 9.1 that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^1,u^2 \in \mathscr {U}^\beta $$\end{document}u1,u2∈Uβ. Thus Assumption 8.1 satisfied. Therefore by Lemma 8.6 we have for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p \in [2,\infty )$$\end{document}p∈[2,∞) that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${[}u^1,u^2]_{\mathscr {S}_{p}^{1/2}} \le 0.$$\end{document}[u1,u2]Sp1/2≤0.It follows by the above inequality that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sup _{(t,x) \in [0,1]\times \mathbb {T}}\Vert u^1(t,x) - u^2(t,x)\Vert _{L_{p}} =0,$$\end{document}sup(t,x)∈[0,1]×T‖u1(t,x)-u2(t,x)‖Lp=0,and the desired result follows. □ □

Let Assumption 3.1 hold. The rest of the section is concerned with proving the existence of regularised solutions in the class \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {U}^{1+\frac{\alpha }{4}}$$\end{document}U1+α4. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(b^n)_{n \in \mathbb {N}} \subset C^\infty $$\end{document}(bn)n∈N⊂C∞ such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$b^n \rightarrow b$$\end{document}bn→b in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C^\alpha $$\end{document}Cα. Suppose that for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}n∈N, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^n$$\end{document}un is the classical mild solution of the SPDE10.1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} (\partial _t - \Delta )u^n&= b^n(u^n) + \sigma (u^n)\xi , \qquad u^n(0,\cdot )&= u(0,\cdot ). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(∂t-Δ)un=bn(un)+σ(un)ξ,un(0,·)=u(0,·).We call \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u^n)_{n \in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(un)n∈N the sequence of approximate solutions, and for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(t,x) \in [0,1]\times \mathbb {T}$$\end{document}(t,x)∈[0,1]×T we define the corresponding approximate drift term and approximate noise term respectively by\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}D_t^{u^n}(x)&:= \int _0^t \int _{\mathbb {T}}p_{t-r}(x,y)b^n(u^n(r,y))dydr,\\ V^{u^n}_t(x)&:= \int _0^t \int _{\mathbb {T}}p_{t-r}(x,y)\sigma (u^n(r,y))\xi (dy,dr). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Dtn(x):=∫0t∫Tpt-r(x,y)bn(un(r,y))dydr,Vtn(x):=∫0t∫Tpt-r(x,y)σ(un(r,y))ξ(dy,dr).By Lemma 9.1 we have for all p \ge 1p≥1, that10.2\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \sup _{n \in \mathbb {N}}[D^{u^n}]_{\mathscr {V}_p^{1+\alpha /4}} <\infty . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}supn∈N[Dn]Vp1+α/4<∞.

Lemma 10.2

(Convergence of the approximate drift and noise terms) Let Assumption 3.1 hold, and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p \in [1,\infty )$$\end{document}p∈[1,∞) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon \in (0,\frac{1}{2})$$\end{document}ε∈(0,12). Then the sequences \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(D^{u^n})_{n \in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(Dn)n∈N, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(V^{u^n})_{n \in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(Vn)n∈N are convergent in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\varepsilon }{2},\frac{1}{2} - \varepsilon }([0,1]\times \mathbb {T}, L_p)$$\end{document}C14-ε2,12-ε([0,1]×T,Lp).

Proof

Assume without loss of generality that p > 2p>2. By Corollary 7.5 (with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta = 1+\frac{\alpha }{4}$$\end{document}β=1+α4), Lemma 8.6, and keeping in mind (10.2) , we have10.3\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\sup _{(t,x) \in [0,1]\times \mathbb {T}}\Vert D_t^{u^n}(x) - D_t^{u^m}(x)\Vert _{L_p}\nonumber \\&\quad =\sup _{(t,x) \in [0,1]\times \mathbb {T}}\Big \Vert \int _0^t \int _{\mathbb {T}}p_{t-r}(x,y)\big (b^n(u^n(r,y)) - b^m(u^m(r,y))\big )dydr\Big \Vert _{L_p}\nonumber \\&\quad \lesssim \Vert b^n - b^m\Vert _{C^{\alpha -1}} + [u^n,u^m]_{\mathscr {S}^{1/2}_p[0,1]}\lesssim \Vert b^n- b^m\Vert _{C^\alpha } \longrightarrow 0 \end{aligned}$$\end{document}sup(t,x)∈[0,1]×T‖Dtn(x)-Dtm(x)‖Lp=sup(t,x)∈[0,1]×T‖∫0t∫Tpt-r(x,y)(bn(un(r,y))-bm(um(r,y)))dydr‖Lp≲‖bn-bm‖Cα-1+[un,um]Sp1/2[0,1]≲‖bn-bm‖Cα⟶0as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \rightarrow \infty $$\end{document}n→∞. Moreover by Lemma 9.2 (with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta = 1+\frac{\alpha }{4}$$\end{document}β=1+α4) and by (10.2), we have that10.4\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \sup _{n \in \mathbb {N}}\Vert D^{u^n}\Vert _{C^{1/4,1/2}([0,1]\times \mathbb {T}, L_p)} <\infty . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}supn∈N‖Dn‖C1/4,1/2([0,1]×T,Lp)<∞.By (10.3), (10.4), and by a standard interpolation argument, we can see that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(D^{u^n})_{n \in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(Dn)n∈N is Cauchy in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\varepsilon }{2},\frac{1}{2} - \varepsilon }([0,1]\times \mathbb {T}, L_p)$$\end{document}C14-ε2,12-ε([0,1]×T,Lp).

We proceed with showing that the same is true for the sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(V^n)_{n \in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(Vn)n∈N. To this end note that by the BDG inequality, by the definition of the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {S}_p^{1/2}$$\end{document}Sp1/2-bracket, and by Lemma 8.6 we have10.5\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\sup _{(t,x) \in [0,1]\times \mathbb {T}}\Vert V_t^{u^n}(x) - V_t^{u^m}(x)\Vert _{L_p} \nonumber \\&\quad = \sup _{(t,x) \in [0,1]\times \mathbb {T}}\Big \Vert \int _0^t \int _{\mathbb {T}}p_{t-r}(x,y)\big (\sigma (u^n(r,y)) - \sigma (u^m(r,y))\big )\xi (dy,dr)\Big \Vert _{L_p}\nonumber \\&\quad \lesssim t^{1/4}\Vert u^n - u^m\Vert _{\mathbb {B}([0,1]\times \mathbb {T}, L_p)} \le [u^n,u^m]_{\mathscr {S}_p^{1/2}} \lesssim \Vert b^n - b^m\Vert _{C^{\alpha -1}} \longrightarrow 0 \end{aligned}$$\end{document}sup(t,x)∈[0,1]×T‖Vtn(x)-Vtm(x)‖Lp=sup(t,x)∈[0,1]×T‖∫0t∫Tpt-r(x,y)(σ(un(r,y))-σ(um(r,y)))ξ(dy,dr)‖Lp≲t1/4‖un-um‖B([0,1]×T,Lp)≤[un,um]Sp1/2≲‖bn-bm‖Cα-1⟶0as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n,m \rightarrow \infty $$\end{document}n,m→∞. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma \in (0,\varepsilon )$$\end{document}γ∈(0,ε). Using the BDG inequality and the heat kernel estimates (11.4), (11.5), we can see that for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}n∈N, s,t ∈ [0,1]s,t∈[0,1], \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x,\bar{x} \in \mathbb {T}$$\end{document}x,x¯∈T the following estimates hold:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert V_t^{u^n}(x) - V_t^{u^n}(\bar{x})\Vert _{L_p}^2&\lesssim \int _0^t \int _{\mathbb {T}}(p_{t-r}(x,y) - p_{t-r}(\bar{x},y))^2 dydr \lesssim |x-\bar{x}|^{1-2\gamma },\\ \Vert V_t^{u^n}(x) - V_s^{u^n}(x)\Vert _{L_p}^2&\lesssim \int _0^s \int _{\mathbb {T}}(p_{t-r}(x,y) - p_{s-r}(x,y))^2 dydr\\&\qquad + \int _s^t \int _{\mathbb {T}} p_{t-r}^2(x,y)dydr\\&\lesssim |t-s|^{1/2-\gamma }. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Vtn(x)-Vtn(x¯)‖Lp2≲∫0t∫T(pt-r(x,y)-pt-r(x¯,y))2dydr≲|x-x¯|1-2γ,‖Vtn(x)-Vsn(x)‖Lp2≲∫0s∫T(pt-r(x,y)-ps-r(x,y))2dydr+∫st∫Tpt-r2(x,y)dydr≲|t-s|1/2-γ.Therefore we conclude that10.6\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \sup _{n \in \mathbb {N}} \Vert V^{u^n}\Vert _{C^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\gamma }{2}, \frac{1}{2}-\gamma }([0,1]\times \mathbb {T}, L_p)} <\infty . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}supn∈N‖Vn‖C14-γ2,12-γ([0,1]×T,Lp)<∞.By (10.5), (10.6), and by a standard interpolation argument, we can see that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(V^{n})_{n \in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(Vn)n∈N is also Cauchy in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\varepsilon }{2},\frac{1}{2} - \varepsilon }([0,1]\times \mathbb {T}, L_p)$$\end{document}C14-ε2,12-ε([0,1]×T,Lp), and thus the proof is finished. □ □

Consistently with the above lemmas, we will thus denote\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} {D}^{\tilde{u}} :=\lim _{n \rightarrow \infty }D^{u^n} \quad \text {and}\quad V^{\tilde{u}} := \lim _{n \rightarrow \infty }V^{u^n}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Du~:=limn→∞DnandVu~:=limn→∞Vn,where the limits are taken pointwise in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(t,x)\in [0,1]\times \mathbb {T}$$\end{document}(t,x)∈[0,1]×T, in probability. Moreover, it follows that for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon \in (0, 1/2)$$\end{document}ε∈(0,1/2) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p \in [1, \infty ) $$\end{document}p∈[1,∞) we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${D}^{\tilde{u}},V^{\tilde{u}} \in C^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\varepsilon }{2} , \frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon }([0,1]\times \mathbb {T}, L_p)$$\end{document}Du~,Vu~∈C14-ε2,12-ε([0,1]×T,Lp) and10.7\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lim _{n \rightarrow \infty } \Big ( || {D}^{\tilde{u}} -D^{u^n}\Vert _{C^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\varepsilon }{2}, \frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon }([0,1]\times \mathbb {T}, L_p)}+|| V^{\tilde{u}}-V^{u^n}\Vert _{C^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\varepsilon }{2}, \frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon }([0,1]\times \mathbb {T}, L_p)} \Big )=0 . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}limn→∞(||Du~-Dn‖C14-ε2,12-ε([0,1]×T,Lp)+||Vu~-Vn‖C14-ε2,12-ε([0,1]×T,Lp))=0.Moreover for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(t,x) \in [0,1]\times \mathbb {T}$$\end{document}(t,x)∈[0,1]×T, we define10.8\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tilde{u}(t,x) := P_t u_0(x) + D^{\tilde{u}}_t(x) + V^{\tilde{u}}_t(x). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}u~(t,x):=Ptu0(x)+Dtu~(x)+Vtu~(x).

Lemma 10.3

(Identification of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V^{\tilde{u}}$$\end{document}Vu~) Let Assumption 3.1 hold. The map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{u}: \Omega \times [0,1] \times \mathbb {T} \rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}u~:Ω×[0,1]×T→R defined in (10.8) is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {P}\otimes \mathscr {B}(\mathbb {T})$$\end{document}P⊗B(T)-measurable and for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(t,x) \in [0,1]\times \mathbb {T}$$\end{document}(t,x)∈[0,1]×T, we have with probability one\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V^{\tilde{u}}_t(x) = \int _0^t \int _{\mathbb {T}}p_{t-r}(x,y)\sigma (\tilde{u}(r,y))\xi (dy,dr).$$\end{document}Vtu~(x)=∫0t∫Tpt-r(x,y)σ(u~(r,y))ξ(dy,dr).

Proof

Since for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}n∈N, the random field \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^n$$\end{document}un (which is a classically defined mild solution) is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {P}\otimes \mathscr {B}(\mathbb {T})$$\end{document}P⊗B(T)-measurable, so is the limit \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{u}$$\end{document}u~. By the definitions of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D^{\tilde{u}}$$\end{document}Du~ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V^{\tilde{u}}$$\end{document}Vu~ (see (10.7)), by Fatou’s lemma, and by the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{u}$$\end{document}u~ (see (10.8)) we have for p \ge 2p≥2 that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\Vert V_t^{\tilde{u}}(x) - \int _0^t \int _{\mathbb {T}}p_{t-r}(x,y)\sigma (\tilde{u}(r,y))\xi (dy,dr)\Vert _{L_p} \\&\quad \le \liminf _{n \rightarrow \infty }\Big \Vert \int _0^t \int _{\mathbb {T}}p_{t-r}(x,y)\sigma (u^n(r,y)) - \sigma (\tilde{u}(r,y))\xi (dy,dr)\Big \Vert _{L_p}\\&\quad \lesssim t^{1/4}\liminf _{n \rightarrow \infty }\Vert u^n - \tilde{u}\Vert _{\mathbb {B}([0,1]\times \mathbb {T}, L_p)}\\&\quad \lesssim \lim _{n \rightarrow \infty }\Vert D^{u^n} - D^{\tilde{u}}\Vert _{\mathbb {B}([0,1]\times \mathbb {T}, L_p)} + \lim _{n \rightarrow \infty }\Vert V^{u^n} - V^{\tilde{u}}\Vert _{\mathbb {B}([0,1]\times \mathbb {T}, L_p)}= 0, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Vtu~(x)-∫0t∫Tpt-r(x,y)σ(u~(r,y))ξ(dy,dr)‖Lp≤lim infn→∞‖∫0t∫Tpt-r(x,y)σ(un(r,y))-σ(u~(r,y))ξ(dy,dr)‖Lp≲t1/4lim infn→∞‖un-u~‖B([0,1]×T,Lp)≲limn→∞‖Dn-Du~‖B([0,1]×T,Lp)+limn→∞‖Vn-Vu~‖B([0,1]×T,Lp)=0,and thus the proof is finished. □ □

We proceed with verifying that the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D^{\tilde{u}}$$\end{document}Du~ is not an abuse of notation, i.e. that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D^{\tilde{u}}$$\end{document}Du~ is indeed the drift of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{u}$$\end{document}u~ as prescribed in (3.1). To this end, we will first need to prove the following lemma.

Lemma 10.4

Let Assumption 3.1 hold, and for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}n∈N define random fields \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f^n: \Omega \times [0,1]\times \mathbb {T} \rightarrow \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}fn:Ω×[0,1]×T→R by10.9\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} f^n(t,x) :=D_t^{\tilde{u}}(x) - \int _0^t \int _{\mathbb {T}} p_{t-r}(x,y) b^n(\tilde{u}(r,y))dydr. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}fn(t,x):=Dtu~(x)-∫0t∫Tpt-r(x,y)bn(u~(r,y))dydr.Then for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p \in [1,\infty )$$\end{document}p∈[1,∞) we have that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert f^n\Vert _{C^{\frac{1}{4},\frac{1}{2}}([0,1]\times \mathbb {T}, L_p)} \longrightarrow 0$$\end{document}‖fn‖C14,12([0,1]×T,Lp)⟶0 as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \rightarrow \infty $$\end{document}n→∞.

Proof

To bound the sup norm, we note that by Fatou’s lemma, , Corollary 7.2 (with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g = b^m - b^n$$\end{document}g=bm-bn) and Lemma 9.1, we have that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\Vert f^n\Vert _{\mathbb {B}([0,1]\times \mathbb {T}, L_p)}\\&\quad = \sup _{(t,x) \in [0,T]\times \mathbb {T}}\Big \Vert {D}_t^{\tilde{u}}(x) - \int _0^t \int _{\mathbb {T}} p_{t-r}(x,y) b^n(\tilde{u}(r,y))dydr\Big \Vert _{L_p}\\&\quad \le \liminf _{m \rightarrow \infty }\sup _{(t,x) \in [0,T]\times \mathbb {T}}\Big \Vert \int _0^t \int _{\mathbb {T}}p_{t-r}\big (b^m(u^m(r,y)) - b^n(u^m(r,y))\big )dydr \Big \Vert _{L_p}\\&\quad \lesssim \liminf _{m \rightarrow \infty }\Vert b^m - b^n\Vert _{C^\alpha }(1+[D^{u^m}]_{\mathscr {V}_2^{1+\alpha /4}})(t-s)^{1 + \alpha /4}\lesssim \Vert b - b^n\Vert _{C^\alpha }, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖fn‖B([0,1]×T,Lp)=sup(t,x)∈[0,T]×T‖Dtu~(x)-∫0t∫Tpt-r(x,y)bn(u~(r,y))dydr‖Lp≤lim infm→∞sup(t,x)∈[0,T]×T‖∫0t∫Tpt-r(bm(um(r,y))-bn(um(r,y)))dydr‖Lp≲lim infm→∞‖bm-bn‖Cα(1+[Dm]V21+α/4)(t-s)1+α/4≲‖b-bn‖Cα,and thus10.10\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lim _{n \rightarrow \infty }\Vert f^n\Vert _{\mathbb {B}([0,1]\times \mathbb {T}, L_p)} = 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}limn→∞‖fn‖B([0,1]×T,Lp)=0.Next, we bound the spatial seminorm. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x, \bar{x} \in \mathbb {T}$$\end{document}x,x¯∈T. In the calculation below we will use the definitions of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D^{\tilde{u}}$$\end{document}Du~ \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{u}$$\end{document}u~, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f^n$$\end{document}fn (see (10.7), (10.8), and (10.9)) and the continuity of the approximate drifts, Fatou’s lemma, Corollary 7.3 (with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$g(x) = b^m(x) - b^n(x)$$\end{document}g(x)=bm(x)-bn(x)) and (10.2),\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\sup _{t \in [0,1]}\Vert f^n(t,x) - f^n(t,\bar{x})\Vert _{L_p}\\&\quad =\sup _{t \in [0,1]}\Big \Vert D_t^{\tilde{u}}(x) - D_t^{\tilde{u}}(\bar{x}) - \int _0^t \int _{\mathbb {T}}(p_{t-r}(x,y) - p_{t-r}(\bar{x},y))b^n(\tilde{u}(r,y))dydr\Big \Vert _{L_p}\\&\quad \le \sup _{t \in [0,1]}\liminf _{m \rightarrow \infty }\Big \Vert \int _0^t \int _{\mathbb {T}}\big (p_{t-r}(x,y) - Corollary 10.5

(\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D^{\tilde{u}}$$\end{document}Du~is the drift of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{u}$$\end{document}u~) Let Assumption 3.1 hold. Then the pair \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\tilde{u}, D^{\tilde{u}})$$\end{document}(u~,Du~) satisfies the condition (3.1) from Definition 3.2, that is for any sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(b^n)_{n \in \mathbb {N}}\subset C^\infty $$\end{document}(bn)n∈N⊂C∞ such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$b^n \rightarrow b$$\end{document}bn→b in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C^\alpha $$\end{document}Cα, we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sup _{(t,x) \in [0,T]\times \mathbb {T}}\Big |D_t^{\tilde{u}}(x) - \int _0^t \int _{\mathbb {T}} p_{t-r}(x,y) b^n(\tilde{u}(r,y))dydr\Big | \longrightarrow 0$$\end{document}sup(t,x)∈[0,T]×T|Dtu~(x)-∫0t∫Tpt-r(x,y)bn(u~(r,y))dydr|⟶0in probability as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n \rightarrow \infty $$\end{document}n→∞.

Theorem 10.6

(Existence) Let Assumption 3.1 hold. Then the process \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{u}$$\end{document}u~ is a regularised solution of (1.1) in the class \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {U}^{1 + \alpha /4}$$\end{document}U1+α/4.

Proof

The measurability of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{u}$$\end{document}u~ was shown in Lemma 10.3. Next, by the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{u}$$\end{document}u~ and by Lemma 10.2 we have that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{u} - P_\cdot u_0 \in C^{1/4 - \varepsilon , 1/4 - \varepsilon /2}([0,1]\times \mathbb {T}, L_p)$$\end{document}u~-P·u0∈C1/4-ε,1/4-ε/2([0,1]×T,Lp)for p \ge 1p≥1 and for any \varepsilon >0ε>0. Therefore by Kolmogorov’s continuity theorem, the random field \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{u}(t,x) -P_tu(0,\cdot )(x)$$\end{document}u~(t,x)-Ptu(0,·)(x) is continuous in (t, x). So noting that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P_tu(0,x)$$\end{document}Ptu(0,x) is also continuous in (t, x), it follows that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{u}(t,x)$$\end{document}u~(t,x) is continuous in (t, x). Note moreover that by Corollary 10.5, the pair \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\tilde{u}, {D}^{\tilde{u}})$$\end{document}(u~,Du~) satisfies (3.1). Finally, we observe that by the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{u}$$\end{document}u~ and by Lemma 10.3 the integral equation (3.2) is satisfied. Therefore it is clear that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{u}$$\end{document}u~ is a regularised solution of (1.1). Moreover for all p \ge 1p≥1 we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${[}D^{\tilde{u}}]_{\mathscr {V}_p^{1+\alpha /4}} \le \liminf _{n \rightarrow \infty }[D^{u^n}]_{\mathscr {V}_p^{1+\alpha /4}} \le \sup _{n \in \mathbb {N}}[D^{u^n}]_{\mathscr {V}_p^{1+\alpha /4}} <\infty ,$$\end{document}[Du~]Vp1+α/4≤lim infn→∞[Dn]Vp1+α/4≤supn∈N[Dn]Vp1+α/4<∞,where the last inequality holds by (10.2). Therefore \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{u} \in \mathscr {U}^{1 + \alpha /4}$$\end{document}u~∈U1+α/4, and the proof is finished. □ □
